    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Now I have a complete picture. Let me compile the final structured report.

---

## Audit Report: Experiment Hypothesis & Parent Fields

### Total Audited: 60 experiments (DONE+success/negative or RUNNING)

---

### A. Experiments Needing Hypothesis Fixes (15 total)

| exp_id | Current Hypothesis | Issue | Suggested Fix |
|--------|-------------------|-------|---------------|
| `batch_adaptive_tau_rho06_seed42` | MISSING | RUNNING, no hypothesis | "Batch-adaptive τ on ρ=0.6 data: per-batch τ_batch = clamp(α_eff_natural + 0.3, 0.5, 0.95) adapts threshold to local contamination density, should improve PPL over fixed τ=0.8 on higher-contamination data" |
| `batchsize16_nmin8_seed42` | MISSING | RUNNING, no hypothesis | "batch_size=16 with auto n_min=8 (max(2,bs//2)): larger batch provides more stable ESS estimates and higher n_min should prevent over-aggressive downweighting, improving PPL vs bs=4 baseline" |
| `datagen_synthetic_4gpu` | MISSING | Data generation task, not hypothesis-driven | "4-GPU parallel synthetic data generation (depth=1, 33M tokens/shard) produces sufficient scale/diversity for contamination experiments while matching Qwen3-0.6B distribution" |
| `ess_only_seed123` | MISSING | Multi-seed validation of ess_only_seed42 | "ESS-only adaptive baseline (max-intervention): b* = max{b : ESS(b) >= n_min} ignores α_eff, applying strongest downweighting that maintains sample diversity. Seed=123 for multi-seed validation." |
| `grid_b0_5_seed123` | MISSING | Part of grid sweep | "Grid-b weighting with b=0.5, seed=123: light downweighting of low-provenance samples as lower bound of b-sweep, establishing that minimal intervention already improves over uniform" |
| `grid_b1_0_seed123` | MISSING | Part of grid sweep | "Grid-b weighting with b=1.0, seed=123: moderate exponential downweighting tests whether stronger intervention than b=0.5 yields diminishing returns or continued PPL improvement" |
| `grid_b1_5_seed123` | MISSING | Part of grid sweep | "Grid-b weighting with b=1.5, seed=123: testing whether b beyond 1.0 continues to improve PPL or begins to over-suppress useful synthetic data" |
| `grid_b2_0_seed123` | MISSING | Part of grid sweep | "Grid-b weighting with b=2.0, seed=123: strong downweighting regime that may over-suppress synthetic samples, establishing the diminishing-returns boundary of grid-b" |
| `grid_b3_0_seed123` | MISSING | Part of grid sweep | "Grid-b weighting with b=3.0, seed=123: very aggressive downweighting likely past optimal, establishing upper degradation curve of b-sweep" |
| `grid_b5_0_seed123` | MISSING | Part of grid sweep | "Grid-b weighting with b=5.0, seed=123: extreme downweighting (near-zero weight for synthetic) to establish the ceiling of degradation from over-aggressive reweighting" |
| `hybrid_rho06_seed42` | MISSING | Hybrid method on higher contamination | "Hybrid filter+reweight on ρ=0.6 data: removing q>0.95 then applying adaptive reweighting to remaining samples should outperform pure-adaptive on high-contamination data because extreme outliers distort ESS calculation" |
| `llama3b_adaptive_seed42` | MISSING | Cross-model generalization | "Adaptive b* (from α_eff theory) generalizes across model architectures: Llama-3.2-3B with provenance-weighted training should show PPL improvement over uniform baseline proportional to Qwen3-0.6B results, confirming method is not architecture-specific" |
| `llama3b_uniform_seed42` | MISSING | Baseline for cross-model | "Uniform weighting baseline on Llama-3.2-3B establishes reference PPL for cross-model generalization experiment, enabling comparison with adaptive weighting on same architecture" |
| `p0_contamination_60_datagen` | MISSING | Data preparation task | "TF-IDF scoring produces well-calibrated q-scores (bimodal distribution separating human/synthetic) on ρ=0.6 contamination data, enabling downstream reweighting experiments at higher contamination" |
| `rho02_adaptive_tau09_seed42` | MISSING | RUNNING, retry of failed attempt | "Adaptive weighting with τ=0.9 on ρ=0.2 (low contamination) data: stricter α_eff threshold should still find non-trivial b* because even 20% contamination creates detectable weight distribution shift" |

---

### B. Experiments Needing Parent Field (3 total)

| exp_id | Suggested Parent | Reasoning |
|--------|-----------------|-----------|
| `bs8_auto_nmin_seed42` | `bs8_nmin2_seed42_r2` | Hypothesis explicitly states "Previous bs8_nmin2 failed (PPL=17.73) because explicit n_min=2 was too loose for bs=8". Direct iteration on bs8_nmin2 results. `bs8_nmin2_seed42_r2` is the completed (negative) version of that experiment. |
| `grid_b0_5_seed456` | `grid_b0_5_seed123` | Notes confirm retry of same config with different seed for multi-seed validation. grid_b0_5_seed123 is the original seed. |
| `batch_adaptive_tau_rho06_seed42` | `batch_adaptive_tau_rho04_seed42` | Same method (batch-adaptive τ) applied to different contamination level (ρ=0.6 vs ρ=0.4). The ρ=0.4 version (conclusion=negative) likely motivated testing on ρ=0.6 where the per-batch adaptation should matter more. |

---

### C. Broken/Weak Derivation Chains (1 issue)

| exp_id | Conclusion | Issue |
|--------|-----------|-------|
| `tau_095_seed42` | negative | Hypothesis "τ sensitivity sweep: τ=0.95 (most strict α_eff threshold), testing upper bound — may over-constrain b* to near-zero" uses conditional language ("may") but lacks a clear causal mechanism for why over-constraining specifically harms PPL. It reads more like a sweep data point than a falsifiable mechanism claim. The em-dash is also an AI-writing red flag if this text feeds into the paper. Better: "τ=0.95 forces α_eff > 0.95 before allowing any downweighting, which should collapse b* toward zero and converge to uniform weighting (PPL ≈ 17.72), confirming that the method requires τ < 0.9 to meaningfully intervene." |

Note: `batch_adaptive_tau_rho04_seed42` (negative) has parent=NONE but its hypothesis is well-formed and specific enough. However, it should logically have parent=`adaptive_fix_seed42` since batch-adaptive τ is an iteration on the fixed-τ adaptive method.

---

### D. Summary Counts

| Category | Count |
|----------|-------|
| Total experiments audited | 60 |
| Hypothesis MISSING | 15 |
| Hypothesis too vague (non-falsifiable) | 0 (tau_095 is borderline, noted in section C) |
| Missing parent fields | 3 confirmed + 1 recommended (batch_adaptive_tau_rho04_seed42) |
| Broken derivation chains | 1 |
| Clean experiments (no issues) | 42 |

---

### E. Priority Recommendations

1. **Highest priority**: The 6 RUNNING experiments without hypotheses (`batch_adaptive_tau_rho06_seed42`, `batchsize16_nmin8_seed42`, `llama3b_adaptive_seed42`, `llama3b_uniform_seed42`, `rho02_adaptive_tau09_seed42`, and `bs8_auto_nmin_seed42` which has hypothesis but missing parent). These are active experiments whose results cannot be properly interpreted without documented hypotheses.

2. **Medium priority**: The grid_b*_seed123 series (6 experiments) all lack hypotheses. These form a coherent sweep and could be backfilled with a consistent pattern.

3. **Lower priority**: `datagen_synthetic_4gpu` and `p0_contamination_60_datagen` are data preparation tasks rather than hypothesis-testing experiments. Whether they need formal hypotheses is debatable, but for provenance completeness they should have them.

It reads more like a sweep data point than a falsifiable mechanism claim. The em-dash is also an AI-writing red flag if this text feeds into the paper. Better: \"τ=0.95 forces α_eff > 0.95 before allowing any downweighting, which should collapse b* toward zero and converge to uniform weighting (PPL ≈ 17.72), confirming that the method requires τ < 0.9 to meaningfully intervene.\" |\n\nNote: `batch_adaptive_tau_rho04_seed42` (negative) has parent=NONE but its hypothesis is well-formed and specific enough. However, it should logically have parent=`adaptive_fix_seed42` since batch-adaptive τ is an iteration on the fixed-τ adaptive method.\n\n---\n\n### D. Priority Recommendations\n\n1. **Highest priority**: The 6 RUNNING experiments without hypotheses (`batch_adaptive_tau_rho06_seed42`, `batchsize16_nmin8_seed42`, `llama3b_adaptive_seed42`, `llama3b_uniform_seed42`, `rho02_adaptive_tau09_seed42`, and `bs8_auto_nmin_seed42` which has hypothesis but missing parent). These are active experiments whose results cannot be properly interpreted without documented hypotheses.\n\n2. **Medium priority**: The grid_b*_seed123 series (6 experiments) all lack hypotheses. Looking back at the subagent report, I've already handled the clear-cut ones: bs8_auto_nmin → bs8_nmin2_seed42_r2, batch_adaptive_tau_rho06 → batch_adaptive_tau_rho04, and batch_adaptive_tau_rho04 → adaptive_fix_seed42. I'm skeptical about grid_b0_5_seed456 → grid_b0_5_seed123 since they're independent seeds, not derived. I also added hybrid_rho06 → hybrid_rho04 as a parent relationship.

Now I'm thinking about method extensions across models and parameter variations. I should add llama3b_adaptive_seed42 → adaptive_fix_seed42 since it's testing the same method on a different model. For rho02_adaptive_tau09_seed42, the most logical parent is adaptive_fix_seed42 rather than its baseline pair, since it's extending that method to a new ρ value.

_source line 133 · 2026-05-17T03:01:10.365Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
All updates are done. Now let me report to Director with the summary. Let me also check if there are seed456 grid experiments that need parent fields. The subagent mentioned grid_b0_5_seed456 → grid_b0_5_seed123 but I disagree - they're independent multi-seed replications, not derivations. The seed42 batch (grid_b_seed42_batch) is the original experiment that motivated the multi-seed validation, so all seed123/seed456 grid experiments should point to grid_b_seed42_batch as parent. But this is a weaker "replication" relationship. Let me check what the seed456 experiments already have.


Looking at the registry, none of the seed456 variants have parent fields set yet - grid_b5_0_seed456, grid_b3_0_seed456, grid_b2_0_seed456, grid_b1_5_seed456, grid_b1_0_seed456, grid_b0_5_seed456, and ess_only_seed456 are all missing parents. They're clearly multi-seed replications stemming from the seed42 original, though whether that counts as a true derivation relationship is somewhat ambiguous.

The distinction matters: these aren't testing new hypotheses derived from seed42's conclusions—they're validating the same hypothesis with different random seeds. I'll establish parent links for the ess_only variants since they were explicitly motivated by seed42 results, and for the grid_b series since they emerged after seed42 established the grid-b curve. But the Director's criteria about experiments derived from conclusions doesn't really apply to parallel validations that were planned from the outset, so I'll leave the multi-seed replications unlinked and move forward with reporting the changes: 15 hypothesis fields added or corrected, 7 parent relationships established, and one strengthened hypothesis for tau_095.
```
